- Codec.SysV: images now mount on the real Linux kernel (mount -t sysv) and the kernel reads our files byte-exact — validated under the Alpine qemu guest (was rejected before) # SysVWriter/Reader/Modifier: place the superblock at block 0 + BLOCK_SIZE/2 (offset 512), not block 1 (offset 1024). The Linux sysv driver's detect_sysv reads the superblock struct at bh->b_data + BLOCK_SIZE/2, so s_magic must land at byte 1016 (512+504); at offset 1024 the kernel never found it # SysVWriter: s_isize is the FIRST DATA ZONE (FirstInodeBlock + ilist blocks), not the ilist size. The kernel reads it as s_firstdatazone and rejects the mount when s_firstdatazone < s_firstinodezone (=2); our old value of 1 always failed. Reader/Modifier reinterpret s_isize accordingly (ilist size = s_isize - FirstInodeBlock) * SysVFormatDescriptor: magic signature offset 1528 -> 1016 to match the relocated superblock; SysV tests updated to the new offsets (7f5ddb9)
- Codec.SquashFs: images now mount on the real Linux kernel (mount -t squashfs) and the kernel reads our files byte-exact — validated under the Alpine qemu guest # SquashFsWriter: pad the image to a 4 KiB device boundary (as mksquashfs does). An unpadded sub-sector image (e.g. 234 bytes for two tiny files) presents a zero-sector block device, so the kernel's first superblock read failed with EIO ("SQUASHFS error: Failed to read block 0x0: -5"). bytes_used in the superblock stays the true unpadded size (afcdd3e)
- Codec.Udf: images now mount on the real Linux kernel (mount -t udf) and the kernel reads our files byte-exact — validated under the Alpine qemu guest (was rejected at every layer before) # UdfWriter LVD: emit the mandatory "*OSTA UDF Compliant" domain EntityID + OSTA CS0 character set, a Type-1 partition map (map_table_length=6, number_of_partition_maps=1) and an implementation EntityID; without the domain id the kernel reports "Not OSTA UDF compliant logical volume descriptor" and without the map "No partition found" # UdfWriter partition descriptor: set partition_flags=allocated, partition_number=0, "+NSR02" contents EntityID and access_type so the partition map resolves # UdfWriter FSD: emit the OSTA domain EntityID + CS0 charsets and interchange levels # UdfWriter File Entry: set ICB strategy type 4 (0 → "unsupported strategy type") and move file_link_count to the ECMA-167 offset 48 (was 28, inside the ICB tag — the kernel saw link count 0 and failed udf_iget on the root directory) (cdee5f9)
- Test harness: run the external filesystem conformance + post-mutation suites natively on a Linux/POSIX host, not only via WSL on Windows. FsInteropToolbox (DetectWsl/RunWsl/WinToWsl) and the per-fs WSL helpers (F2fs/Jfs/ReiserFs) now execute the Linux fsck/mkfs tools directly through /bin/bash when the host is not Windows; Windows behaviour is unchanged (gated on OperatingSystem.IsWindows) * This lights up 104 conformance checks + 14 add/remove/modify post-mutation r/w checks on Linux that previously skipped — validating ext/xfs/btrfs/f2fs/jfs/reiserfs/minix/ntfs/iso/squashfs/cramfs/bfs writers against the real host tools, and confirming F2fs/Jfs/ReiserFs stay fsck-clean after mutation # WinToWsl returns the bare POSIX path on a non-Windows host (a single-quoted form broke the direct-exec qemu-img container tests) (1034a73)
- Codec.Hammer2 WRITE now produces files the DragonFly kernel reads byte-exact — full read/write. Validated via mount_hammer2 @<label> + cksum on hello/readme (embedded data) + big.bin (4000B out-of-line data block) in a directory large enough to spill into a blockref-indirect block # Hammer2Writer: blockref modify_tid must be non-zero (set to mirror_tid) — the live kernel treats modify_tid==0 as an uninitialised/empty chain and skips it during directory lookup and readdir, so files were on-disk (visible to hammer2 show) but invisible to the mounted filesystem # Hammer2Writer: a blockref-indirect must never span the full 64-bit keyspace (keybits 64); the kernel caps keybits at 63 (1<<64 is undefined → empty range). A directory whose entries straddle bit 63 (inode brefs keyed by inum, dirent brefs keyed by dirhash with bit 63 set) now splits at bit 63 into a low-half and high-half keybits=63 indirect, matching the kernel's own layout # Hammer2Writer: indirect blockref leaf_count carries the number of leaf brefs beneath it (was 0) (1a566d5)
- Codec.Hammer WRITE now produces files the DragonFly kernel reads byte-exact — full read/write. Validated via mount_hammer + cksum on hello/readme/mid(5000B)/big(40000B multi-block); image passes hammer show (all elements GOOD) and hammer checkmap (0 errors) # HammerWriter: B-Tree leaf node mirror_tid must be >= every element's create_tid (was 0) — otherwise the kernel flags BADMIRRORTID and the ASOF read cursor prunes the node # HammerWriter: directory-entry data belongs in the META zone (zone 9), not small-data (zone 11) — a mixed-zone big-block fails kernel blockmap validation and checkmap # HammerWriter: directory-entry embedded localization is the target's pseudofs localization (0 for PFS#0), not the INODE app bit — the kernel derives ip->obj_localization from it and reads file data with (obj_localization | MISC); the wrong value made the data query localization 3 instead of 2 so the read found no data record (zero-fill) while the inode lookup still succeeded # HammerWriter: full 16 KB data blocks must live in the LARGE_DATA zone (zone 10), sub-16 KB tails in SMALL_DATA (zone 11) — the kernel's direct-read path asserts hammer_is_zone_large_data() and panics on an aligned full-buffer block stored in small-data * HammerRoundTripTests: add a 40000-byte multi-block file exercising large+small data zones (033e3b2)
- Codec.Hammer/Codec.Hammer2 readers: walk the on-disk tree to recover real files — HammerReader (vol0 B-Tree → inode/direntry/data records) and Hammer2Reader (volume header → super-root → PFS → blockref tree); both verified byte-exact against images the DragonFly kernel itself wrote (newfs_hammer/newfs_hammer2 + populate), including subdirectories + HammerFormatDescriptor.List/Extract surface the actual files via HammerReader (mirrors the wired HAMMER2 descriptor) instead of only the volume-header bundle + HammerWriter/Hammer2Writer materialise per-file inode + directory-entry + data records that round-trip byte-exact through our own readers (Hammer/Hammer2 round-trip + descriptor tests) + Hammer/Hammer2 kernel-interop read tests (gated on /tmp/hammer{,2}_ref.img): recover frombsd.txt/inner.txt from a real DragonFly-written volume + UfsBsdInteropTests: our UfsReader extracts a FreeBSD newfs+populated UFS1 image byte-exact — frombsd.txt, sub/inner.txt, and a 4.5 MB big.bin spanning single-indirect blocks * UfsFormatDescriptor accepted-inputs text corrected to the real capability (multiple cylinder groups, nested directories, direct + single-indirect files) ! Hammer kernel-WRITE: mounted image lists files with correct names/sizes but the kernel reads the data region as zero-fill — full kernel-readable data still needs zone-2 blockmap allocation ! Hammer2 kernel-WRITE: image mounts clean but the kernel walks an empty root — surfacing our files to the kernel still needs freemap allocation + blockref-indirect wiring (ab5b89c)
- Codec.Hammer2: HAMMER2 (DragonFly BSD) writer — produces images the real kernel mounts cleanly (mount_hammer2 rc=0, "no recovery needed", root lists, clean umount; validated under DragonFly 6.4) + Hammer2 volume header at offset 0 (slot #0 of 4): version 2, fstype UUID, allocator_beg past boot(8M@4M)/aux(8M@12M)/topo-reserved(4M@20M), volu_loff[0]=0 and [1..63]=-1 (kernel rejects non-(-1) unused volume slots) + Hammer2 topology: SUPROOT inode → two PFS-MASTER inodes (@LOCAL default + @<label>), blockrefs sorted ascending by name_key, empty root dirs (matches newfs_hammer2) + Hammer2Crc: iSCSI CRC-32C (poly 0x82F63B78) for the three volume-header iCRCs — SECT1 stamped before SECT0 (SECT0 range covers the SECT1 field) — plus seeded xxHash64 (XXH_HAMMER2_SEED) blockref checks and hammer2_dirhash name_key * Hammer2FormatDescriptor: implements IArchiveCreatable / CanCreate / Create * ExternalFsInteropTests: Hammer2_OurImage_DragonFlyHammer2Info now drives the real writer * EndToEndInteropTests: exclude .hammer2 from the self-round-trip sweep (multi-hundred-MB image) ! Hammer2 root directories are empty (as newfs_hammer2) — AddFile records the label surface but does not yet materialise DIRENT blockrefs / per-file child inodes; freemap left lazy (kernel builds it); only volume-header slot #0 written (20dada6)
- HAMMER (v1, DragonFly BSD) writer: HammerWriter produces a genuine mountable HAMMER volume — volume header, two-layer freemap blockmap, UNDO/REDO FIFO (512MB floor), root B-Tree + root inode + PFS#0 MASTER record, sparse buffer-cache I/O. HammerCrc: CRC-32 (poly 0xEDB88320) and version-gated CRC-32C (0x82F63B78) per hammer_datacrc. Descriptor gains IArchiveCreatable/CanCreate. * Verified under real DragonFly BSD 6.4 (qemu): mount_hammer /dev/vbd1 mounts CLEAN (rc=0), 'hammer info' reports Label/Version 7/Volumes/Inodes (rc=0), root dir lists, umount clean * .hammer excluded from the generic SelfRoundTrip (filesystem image, like .ufs/.xfs) ! AddFile records the label/inode surface but does not yet materialize directory entries + per-file inodes (root dir empty, as newfs_hammer leaves it); single-volume only (f392911)

- Pak + Shar promoted to in-place IArchiveModifiable (CanModify) * Pak Add/Remove delegate to PakInPlaceModifier → ArcModifier (shared ARC entry-chain + 0x1A 0x00 EOA marker) * Shar Add overwrites the trailing 'exit 0\n' sentinel with the new heredoc/uudecode block + a fresh sentinel; Remove throws NotSupported (heredoc bodies can carry delimiter-lookalike text) + Tar / Cpio / Ar in-place LOCK tests: pre-existing bytes before the textbook terminator (two zero blocks / TRAILER!!! / EOF) are byte-identical after Add + Pak / Shar in-place tests: byte-identity invariant + Add/Remove round-trip + descriptor advertises CanModify + IArchiveModifiable (8219e67)

- Ico in-place R/W: IcoInPlaceModifier mutates ICONDIR + ICONDIRENTRY table directly; Add appends payload at EOF, inserts 16-byte dir entry, patches existing offsets by +16, every payload's byte content stays verbatim; Remove collapses the dir slot + payload bytes, patches survivor offsets down by 16 (and by payload-length for payloads past the deleted one), physically wipes removed bytes + Eml in-place R/W: EmlInPlaceModifier splices the multipart body without rewriting surviving parts; Add appends a fresh attachment part (base64 transfer encoding, 76-char line wrap) immediately before the closing --boundary-- delimiter so every byte before the splice point stays at its original offset; Remove deletes the byte range of a named attachment between two boundary markers; both reject single-part messages with NotSupportedException because promoting to multipart would cascade through the top-level Content-Type * IcoFormatDescriptor + EmlFormatDescriptor: implement IArchiveModifiable + advertise CanModify, Add/Remove route through the new modifiers * DtbFormatDescriptor / MoFormatDescriptor: stay R-only with honest demotion — Description now names the specific spec field cascade that blocks in-place mutation (DTB: 40-byte FDT header totalsize + off_dt_struct + off_dt_strings + size_dt_struct + size_dt_strings shift with every property add/remove; MO: 28-byte header numStrings + table offsets sit BEFORE the key/value pools, so growing the descriptor tables shifts every pool byte and invalidates every (off) field already written) + IcoInPlaceModifyTests (12): byte-identical payload preservation on Add, +16 offset patch on existing entries, BMP→DIB conversion path, sequence Add+Remove restores original entry list, removed PNG signature physically wiped (no forensic survivor), descriptor wiring round-trip through IArchiveModifiable + EmlInPlaceModifyTests (11): closing delimiter preserved, new attachment decodable through existing reader, original attachment bytes preserved across Add, bytes before splice point byte-identical, single-part Add throws NotSupported, removed base64 payload bytes physically wiped, Add+Remove restores byte-length parity, descriptor accepts both "attachments/<name>" and bare filename forms + DtbInPlaceModifyTests + MoInPlaceModifyTests: lock the honest-demotion contract (no CanModify, no IArchiveModifiable, Description names the blocking fields) (4fce4dd)
- Pfs0 + Gemdos + GsOs + Mfs1 promoted to in-place R/W (CanModify) + Pfs0InPlaceModifier: flat header + entry table + string table + data   region rewritten in place; replace-by-name semantics; data region   re-laid out so removed payloads physically disappear + GemdosInPlaceModifier: delegates to FAT12 (patches 0x60 BRA.S jump   byte for FatReader, restores it post-mutation); Add re-packs through   FatWriter, Remove via FatRemover + GsOsWriter: emits 2IMG container (creator code "CWB!", image_format=1)   wrapping a ProDOS payload via ProDosWriter; honest scope on the   Description for non-ProDOS-ordered payloads + GsOsInPlaceModifier: rebuilds inner ProDOS payload through   ProDosReader+ProDosWriter while preserving the 64-byte 2IMG header   byte-for-byte (rebuild path avoids a pre-existing ProDosModifier   slot-1 / volume-header byte-offset collision that corrupts the   bitmap pointer and total-blocks fields after the first added file) + Mfs1Writer: real DFS-tier catalog emitter — sector 0 names + sector   1 metadata with packed-high-bits encoding, files contiguous from   sector 2 onwards, descending start-sector catalog sort per DFS   convention; 200 KB default geometry (80 tracks x 10 sectors) + Mfs1InPlaceModifier: re-packs through Mfs1Writer; outer sector count   preserved; removed payload bytes don't survive + 4 InPlaceModify test fixtures (44 tests): Add/Remove/MutateThenExtract   roundtrips, header / jump-byte / sector-count preservation, payload   wipe verification, interface routing * Mfs1FormatDescriptor: read-only -> R/W (CanCreate + CanModify);   IArchiveCreatable + IArchiveModifiable implemented * GsOsFormatDescriptor: read-only stub -> R/W (CanCreate + CanModify);   IArchiveCreatable + IArchiveModifiable implemented; List/Extract now   walks the inner ProDOS catalog for image_format=1 and falls back to   opaque blob for HFS/DOS-3.3 payloads * GemdosFormatDescriptor: + CanModify + IArchiveModifiable; Add/Remove   routed through GemdosInPlaceModifier * Pfs0FormatDescriptor: + CanModify + IArchiveModifiable; Add/Remove   routed through Pfs0InPlaceModifier * Mfs1StubBehaviorTests + GsOsStubBehaviorTests: capability assertions   inverted from "no CanCreate/CanModify" to "+CanCreate +CanModify";   opaque-fallback path retained for non-ProDOS / unparseable inputs (91a00b6)
- Cdi + Nrg + Mdf R/W: in-place sector rewrite at fixed offsets via IArchiveModifiable (1dcc267)
- OpenVms (Files-11 ODS-2): WORM writer + in-place R/W Add/Remove/Replace (87e816a)
- BinCue R/W: in-place sector rewrite at fixed offsets via IArchiveModifiable (109bd97)
- Ocfs2 true in-place R/W: Add/Replace/Remove via Ocfs2InPlaceModifier — O(touched bytes) random-access I/O against global bitmap data block, root dir dinode inline area, file dinode block, and contiguous data clusters. Untouched blocks remain byte-identical * Ocfs2FormatDescriptor.Add/Remove now route through Ocfs2InPlaceModifier first; fall back to ModifyRebuilder only when the in-place path hits its honest scope (subdir paths, extent-backed root dir, full inline area, no free clusters) + Ocfs2InPlaceModifyTests: 28 tests covering Add/Replace/Remove untouched-block invariants, bitmap update + freed-bit accounting, multi-cluster files, wipe-on-remove forensic check, mutate-then-extract roundtrip, descriptor-routing, and error cases (duplicate name, subdir path, oversize replace, invalid image, non-seekable stream) ! MVP scope (documented in Description + class XML): root-directory mutations only. Subdirectory mutation, extent-backed root B-tree splits, DLM/heartbeat lockdown, and multi-node cluster semantics deferred — the rebuild path still handles them (af6314f)
- Aomei .adi/.afi WORM -> R/W via trailing BR_IMAGE_INDEX VDB-entry append (3237372)
- Coherent FS true in-place R/W via V7-style inode + zone mutation + CoherentInPlaceModifier — canonical Add/Replace/Remove surface over a Coherent image stream; Add inserts via free-inode scan + free-zone reachability scan across direct + single-indirect + double-indirect tiers; Replace rewrites payload bytes at the inode's existing data zone offsets when the new size fits and falls back to Remove+Add otherwise; Remove wipes data + indirect pointer blocks + dirent + inode slot * CoherentFormatDescriptor — Add/Remove now delegate to CoherentInPlaceModifier with no rebuild fallback so capacity limits surface honestly; Description rewritten to spell out the in-place semantic and the root-only scope * CoherentRwTests.Descriptor_Add_FallsBackToRebuildOnInodeExhaustion → Descriptor_Add_SurfacesIoExceptionOnInodeExhaustion: lock the new no-rebuild contract + CoherentInPlaceModifyTests — Add (untouched data/superblock byte-identical, empty input no-op), Remove (zone + dirent wipe, missing-name no-op + image-unchanged, magic preserved), Replace (fits→in-place same-length boundary + other-file blocks unchanged + size-field path, missing→falls through to Add, larger→realloc fallback), Add+Replace+Remove round-trip, bad-magic validation across all three operations, descriptor wiring ! Subdirectory mutation deferred — Add/Replace/Remove operate on the root directory only; multi-component names are flattened to their leaf before lookup (d474eda)

- Ghost 3.0+ WORM -> R/W via record-stream append (GhostInPlaceModifier) (807e22b)
- Ubifs promoted from WORM to true in-place R/W via journal-head append + UbifsWriter: emits superblock + master + linear log of inode/dentry/zlib-compressed data nodes (kernel UBIFS LPT/TNC commit pipeline deliberately out of scope — self-round-trip only, not kernel-mountable) + UbifsInPlaceModifier: Add / Replace / Remove append fresh INO + DENT + DATA nodes at the journal head with monotonically increasing sqnum; existing committed nodes stay byte-identical at their original offsets — only the trailing 0xFF padding of the journal-head LEB gets overwritten; tombstone DENT (inum=0) for Remove; 8-byte node alignment and per-LEB node-no-straddle invariant preserved * UbifsFileReader: sqnum-aware merge for INO / DENT / DATA — highest-sqnum entry per key wins, tombstones suppress earlier dentries (matches kernel TNC lookup semantic after journal replay) * UbifsFormatDescriptor: implements IArchiveCreatable + IArchiveModifiable; Capabilities adds CanCreate + CanModify; Add / Remove route through UbifsInPlaceModifier, Create delegates to UbifsWriter * Description spells out the R/W scope (Add/Replace/Remove append at journal head, committed bytes preserved) and the deferred-scope honesty (no TNC/LPT commit → not kernel-mountable) + UbifsInPlaceModifyTests (10 tests): Add preserves committed bytes + appends a new file; Replace keeps old DATA nodes byte-identical and the reader picks higher-sqnum content; Remove appends tombstone and the reader drops the entry; replace-via-Add reuses the existing inode; multi-LEB straddling large file round-trips; mutate-then-extract through the descriptor surface composes all three ops; rejects non-seekable streams and unknown-name Replace + UbifsWriterTests: WORM self-round-trip for Create (single + multi-file) * UbifsReaderTests.Descriptor_IsHonestlyReadOnly → Descriptor_AdvertisesRwScope: now locks the IArchiveCreatable + IArchiveModifiable advertisement and CanCreate + CanModify capability bits (5de3ed3)
- Macrium Reflect X promoted from WORM to R/W via rebuild-based modify (e69d745)
- Norton Ghost (modern 3.0+) R/W via IArchiveModifiable — promotes the FE EF record container from WORM (Create-only) to R/W (Create + Add/Remove/Replace) + GhostModifier: rebuild-based Add/Remove/Replace helpers that preserve the source image's compression mode (None / FastLZ Z1 / zlib Z3-Z9) and CRC-16 stream cipher state across mutation; in-place replacement keeps Ghost's positional partitionN.bin labels stable when callers overwrite by name; synthetic reader entries (metadata.ini, *.error.txt, dump-*.bin, ghost-image.*.bin) are filtered out so they never round-trip as payloads + GhostFormatDescriptor: implements IArchiveModifiable; Capabilities adds CanModify; interface Add/Remove delegate to GhostModifier with password=null (the IArchiveModifiable contract has no password parameter — encrypted images go through GhostModifier static overloads that accept it explicitly) + GhostReader.HeaderCompression: exposes the parsed file-header compression byte so the rebuild path keeps using the source codec * Description: documents the R/W scope (Add/Remove/Replace) + rebuild-based strategy alongside the existing WORM Create surface + GhostRwTests (24 tests): Add appends + preserves untouched entries + in-place replaces by name; Remove drops named entries + is a no-op for unknown names; Replace swaps payloads + falls through to Add on missing names; FE EF + 0x012F18D8 record framing survives Add and Remove; password- protected images round-trip through Add / Remove / Replace with cipher integrity intact; compression mode is preserved across all four code paths (None / FastLZ / zlib Z3 / zlib Z9); rejects mutation of encrypted images without password, pre-3.0 Ghost 1.x / 2.x dumps, read-only streams and null arguments; metadata.ini is not re-emitted as a payload * GhostFormatTests: descriptor-surface gates flipped from "Is.Not.InstanceOf<IArchiveModifiable>" / "CanModify == False" to assert the new R/W surface ! Modify is rebuild-based (read-extract-rebuild) rather than per-record patching — Ghost's compressed-block spans carry no length-of-payload field so patching would have to rewrite every downstream offset; rebuild is the spec-correct option here and matches the ZIP / NTFS / Btrfs approach acknowledged by the project memory (361e109)
- AdvFs WORM: emit clean-room storage-domain layout (RBMT page 0 cookie + DMN/VD/MATTR fields + CWB-AdvFS-WB file table at byte offset 132 of the page); reader extended to parse the file-table extension and round-trip caller files end-to-end + OpenVms WORM (ODS-2): emit a real Files-11 home block at LBN 1 with DECFILE11A magic at +0x1E8, structure level 0x0202, cluster size 1, owner UIC [1,1], plus a CWB-OVMS-WB file table at LBN 2 carrying caller files into a flat data area at byte offset 8192. Honest scope: INDEXF.SYS / BITMAP.SYS / checksum1 deferred — emitted volume is not OpenVMS-mountable and the Description says so + AdvFsWormTests / OpenVmsWormTests: round-trip determinism, capability advertisement, single + multi-file payload round-trip, capacity-overflow boundary, name-length boundary, descriptor.Create end-to-end with List+Extract + HammerWormTests / Hammer2WormTests / NssWormTests: lock R-only state with specific writer-gap descriptions — HAMMER1 cluster B-tree + zone blockmap + undo-fifo, HAMMER2 four-redundant volume-data sectors + COW blockref radix + xxHash64, NSS proprietary 'Beast' object record layout never publicly documented * OpenVmsTests.Descriptor_IsHonestlyReadOnly → Descriptor_WormScope_NotModifiable_NotOpenVmsMountable: now locks 'WORM emit + R-only modify' surface and verifies the not-OpenVMS-mountable caveat stays in the Description * HammerFormatDescriptor / Hammer2FormatDescriptor / NssFormatDescriptor: Description now spells out the deferred work so a future agent can see what each WORM gate actually requires (6d59eec)
- WORM writers for 7 pseudo-archive formats: ANI, AppleSingle, DTB, EML, TTC, MO, LNK * descriptors now expose IArchiveCreatable + FormatCapabilities.CanCreate; readers/listings unchanged + AniWriter: RIFF "ACON" wrapper with anih + LIST "fram" of icon subchunks; accepts CUR/ICO inputs only, validates magic 00 00 01/02 00 + AppleSingleWriter: RFC 1740 v2 container, name → entry-id mapping for documented roles, synthetic high-range id for unknown names + DtbWriter: minimal valid FDT v17 — 40-byte BE header + mem-reservation terminator + struct block (root node with spec-required #address-cells/#size-cells + one FDT_PROP per input) + strings block + EmlWriter: RFC 2822 / MIME, multipart/mixed body with per-part base64 transfer encoding, deterministic Message-ID + boundary so identical inputs yield byte-identical messages + TtcWriter: OpenType TTC v1 — 12-byte header + N×u32 offset table + verbatim member SFNT payloads, rejects non-SFNT inputs + MoWriter: GNU gettext binary catalog (little-endian rev 0), HEADER entry sorts first, msgctxt encoded with EOT separator, plurals via NUL separator + LnkWriter: MS-SHLLINK minimal shortcut — 76-byte header + LinkInfo with VolumeID + LocalBasePath + optional WorkingDir/Arguments/IconLocation Unicode StringData blocks + terminator + 7 WriterTests files covering header layout, round-trip through existing reader, descriptor IArchiveCreatable contract, boundary cases (empty inputs, invalid frames, deterministic output) (9b63e95)
- Sfar WORM: stored-mode SfarWriter + IArchiveCreatable, synthetic Filenames.txt manifest round-trips through SfarReader + ApkNativeLibs WORM: IArchiveCreatable delegating to ZipWriter, native_libs/* → lib/* rewrite preserves split-APK shape + AndroidBundle WORM: IArchiveCreatable via ZipWriter, auto-emits placeholder BundleConfig.pb when absent + Esd WORM: IArchiveCreatable delegating to WimWriter (CompressionNone), patches header flags with community-known 0x00100000 ESD marker bit + GameMaker WORM: IArchiveCreatable rebuilds FORM IFF container from chunks/<TAG>.bin inputs, falls back to minimal GEN8 stub + 18 new WORM contract tests across 5 formats (95 total format tests pass) (b0670ea)

- TUX2 WORM writer — single-phase synthetic image: 16-byte header (TUX2FS magic + version + file count) followed by per-file records (u16 name len, UTF-8 name, u32 data len, raw bytes); round-trips through Tux2Reader's documented synthetic header + TUX3 WORM writer — single-version image with documented superblock prefix at offset 4096 (TUX3SUPR magic + birthday/flags/iroot/oroot/aroot/blockbits/volblocks/freeblocks/nextalloc/atomgen/freeatom) plus a sentinel TUX3WORM file table at block 2 (offset 8192) carrying per-file records; no version chain, no atomic-commit log * TUX3 reader picks up the optional TUX3WORM table when present, surfacing per-file entries; legacy linux-tux3 prototype images without the WORM sentinel still surface only as FULL.tux3 + metadata.ini + superblock.bin (HasWormTable=false) * TUX2/TUX3 descriptors now implement IArchiveCreatable with FormatCapabilities.CanCreate; refresh per-descriptor tests for the new capability set + Tux2WriterTests / Tux3WriterTests covering header layout, single-/multi-/empty-/UTF8-named-file round-trips, block-alignment, no-input image emission, legacy-image (no WORM table) compatibility, and ArgumentException paths * UFS / CP/M / HPFS already shipped full WORM (CanCreate + SupportsMultipleEntries + modify) in prior phases — verified state, no changes needed (7a5368d)
- Mbox WORM writer: appends RFC 822 messages with envelope separator + ">From " body byte-stuffing per RFC 4155; descriptor implements IArchiveCreatable + Rpa WORM writer: emits RPA-3.0 header + raw payload region + zlib-compressed protocol-2 pickle index (offset/length XORed with archive key); minimal pickle emitter consumes the same opcode subset the existing parser already handles + AppImage WORM writer: emits a 64-byte ELF64 stub carrying the AI\x02 type-2 marker at e_ident[EI_PAD] followed by a SquashFS payload built via the existing FileSystem.SquashFs writer; descriptor strips synthetic filesystem/ prefix + metadata.ini on Create for clean round-trip + Crate WORM writer: TAR.GZ compound — inputs flow through TarWriter then GZipStream; auto-derives <name-version>/ top directory from supplied Cargo.toml [package] table, falls back to crate-0.0.0 + Pfs0 descriptor Create test: pins the existing Pfs0Writer round-trip via IArchiveCreatable + EndToEndInteropTests: .mbox and .crate added to SelfRoundTrip exclusions — Mbox surfaces synthetic message_NN_<subject>.eml names; Crate requires Cargo.toml-derived top dir for the round-trip verifier (e40a47d)
- Mfs1 WORM writer: Acorn MFS-1 (BBC Master) emits DFS-shaped catalog + contiguous file data, round-trips through the existing reader * Mfs1FormatDescriptor: implements IArchiveCreatable, advertises CanCreate; Description updated to 'R + Create only, modify/defrag deferred' * LittleFs / SmartFs descriptors: keep R-only — Descriptions now document the specific writer work each WORM promotion would need (LittleFs: tag-based metadata-pair commit log with XOR-chained tags + CRC32-tag terminators + mirrored superblock pairs + inline/CTZ data split; SmartFs: per-sector logical-physical mapping table + wear-level sequence counters + directory sector chains + free-sector allocator) and the missing external validator that blocks honest WORM emission * Mfs1StubBehaviorTests: pin WORM (R + CanCreate) rather than R-only; modify/defrag must still NOT appear (cb8023f)
- FileSystem.GsOs WORM emitter — wraps a ProDosWriter volume in the canonical 64-byte 2IMG header (creator code CmpW, ProDOS block order, optional comment block); promoted descriptor from stub to CanCreate / SupportsMultipleEntries / SupportsDirectories with documented spec sources (universal 2IMG spec, ProDOS 8 Technical Reference, Apple IIgs Hardware Reference) + FileSystem.CbmNibble G64 v2 writer — emits the full 35-track / 84-half-track 1541 layout with spec-correct speed-zone table (zones 0..3 by track range), 4-to-5 GCR nibble encoding per the 1541 ROM table, full SYNC + header gap + data gap framing per sector, minimal CBM DOS BAM block at track 18 sector 0 and an 8-entry directory block at track 18 sector 1; G64FormatDescriptor advertises CanCreate * GsOsStubBehaviorTests retitled and re-pinned for the new WORM tier — still asserts CanModify is not advertised (rewrites recompute the 2IMG header so only Create handles them correctly), still pins the opaque-inner-volume read surface (08ee802)

- AOMEI .adi/.afi: VDB entry field byte offsets + BIFT body field offsets + 3 INFO_TYPE_* numeric values reverse-engineered from binary analysis of ImgFile.dll (codename BRCloudv2) + AomeiConstants: per-field byte offsets of BR_IMAGE_INDEX_ENTRY_VDB pinned via three independent code paths in the 32-bit ImgFile.dll: ReadBlock prologue at vma 0x10026060 (xmm0 copies of vdb[0..0xf] and vdb[0x10..0x1f] into [ebp-0x2c] / [ebp-0x1c], psrldq extraction of vdb[0x14] for the pOld malloc, push of [ebp-0x20]/[ebp-0x1c] forming the u64 ImgOffset arg of ReadData); wrapper at 0x10027c91 (movaps [esp+0x50],xmm0 mirroring vdb second half, BRCrc32 call taking [esp+0x54]=OldSize as byte-count, cmp eax,[esp+0x5c]=Crc32 leading to the `Crc32==vdb.Crc32` assert); GetBlock arg-marshalling at 0x10026d8a (movd esi,xmm1 = vdb[0..3] pushed first ⇒ RegNo, psrldq xmm0,4 then movd edx = vdb[4..7] = BlockNo_low, psrldq xmm1,8 then movd ecx = vdb[8..0xb] = BlockNo_high). Resulting layout: RegNo:u32@0x00, BlockNo:u64@0x04, ImgOffset:u64@0x0C, OldSize:u32@0x14, NewSize:u32@0x18, Crc32:u32@0x1C — totals to 0x20 ✓ + AomeiConstants: byte offsets of DataOffInSet:u64 and DataLenInSet:u64 within the 0x674-byte BR_IMAGE_FILE_TAIL pinned via reader-side `mov reg,[edi+0xc80..0xc8c]` at vma 0x100171b0 / 0x100172d4 / 0x10017342 plus the m_Tail base at object offset 0x660 established by the `rep movsd` at 0x10017cf3 in the tail-load routine. Subtracting yields DataLenInSet@+0x620 and DataOffInSet@+0x628 within the tail struct + AomeiConstants: position of the trailing BR_STANDARD_HEADER within the 0x674-byte BIFT pinned at offsets {Reserved:+0x664, Crc32:+0x668, Size:+0x66C, Flag:+0x670} — the tail's standard header sits at the END of the struct (vs the head's start-of-struct placement). Recovered from `cmp [ebp-0x8], 'BIFT'` at 0x10017b9a, `cmp [ebp-0xc], 0x674` at 0x10017bfd, and the CRC verification block at 0x10017c60..0x10017c84 that loads/zeroes [ebp-0x10] before recomputing + AomeiConstants: INFO_TYPE_FLB_SUB_ENTRY_LIST = 0x110 — pinned by `push 0x110` at 0x1000a2d0 immediately preceding the assert-text xref `AddInfo(INFO_TYPE_FLB_SUB_ENTRY_LIST, &m_vSubEntList[0], ...)` at 0x1000a2fd + AomeiConstants: INFO_TYPE_FLB_FILE_DATA_BLOCK_LIST = 0x111 — pinned by `push 0x111` at 0x1000a341 immediately preceding the assert-text xref `AddInfo(INFO_TYPE_FLB_FILE_DATA_BLOCK_LIST, &m_vDataBlockList[0], ...)` at 0x1000a36e. Both resolve the prior {0x110, 0x111, 0x128} candidate set + AomeiConstants: INFO_TYPE_VOLUME_DATA_REGION = 0x109 + sizeof(BR_IMAGE_INFO_VOLUME_DATA_REGION) = 0x30 — pinned by `cmp dword ptr [ebp-0x34], 0x109` at 0x1002554c (Type check) and `cmp eax, 0x30` at 0x10025558 (size check), both jne branches landing on the corresponding sizeof / Region.Header.Type assert-text xrefs at parses the right offsets * AomeiInfoRecord.TypeName: recognises the three newly-pinned INFO_TYPE_* tags (FLB_SUB_ENTRY_LIST, FLB_FILE_DATA_BLOCK_LIST, VOLUME_DATA_REGION) so unknown_0xNNNN no longer `volume_data_region_size=0x30`, and the three new tags added to info_types_enum * AomeiTests.Extract_WritesParsedHeader_OnValidMagic: tail_body_layout substring updated to match the now-pinned offsets consecutive-u64 + fits-inside-BIFT invariants, each of the four BIFT trailing-header positions with the contiguous-and-ends-at-tail-size invariant, the TypeName mapping for the new tags, and the metadata.ini surface format ! Out of scope (documented-TODO): sizeof(BR_IMAGE_INDEX_ENTRY_FDB) — no `cmp [reg+0x18], imm` preamble at the FDB assert site so the byte size remains undecided; the head/tail body bytes between offset 0x10 and the recovered DataLen/DataOff/trailer fields remain unmapped; the on-disk wire layout has NOT been swapped to the 16-byte vendor BR_STANDARD_HEADER so AomeiWriter still emits a wire-incompatible 12-byte alias; AES variant + IV derivation (3f4a3f4)

- Acronis True Image .tibx Stage-3: LSM_LEAF body LZ4-chained-stream decoder + InputItem ItemCommon attribute scanner + Golomb-Rice k=8 codec per binary RE of libarchive3.so + AcronisTibxLsmRecord.DecodeLeafBody: walks the (BE32 zchunk_size, BE32 chunk_size, LZ4-block bytes) chained-stream framing starting at page+0x20 for LSM_LEAF pages with encoding byte 3, decompresses each chunk via Lz4BlockDecompressor (the binary uses LZ4_decompress_safe_continue at libarchive3.so 0x54fb0 with the LZ4 dictionary preserved across chunks; for the all-in-one-chunk case this is identical to LZ4_decompress_safe). Returns a DecodedLeafBody record with the decompressed bytes, a status string (ok / unsupported_encoding / empty_body / lz4_chunk_error / buffer_underrun / len_mismatch), and the LZ4 chunk count + AcronisTibxLsmRecord.ScanForItemCommonAttributes: forensic-grade scan of the decompressed leaf body for InputItem ItemCommon (id 0x10) attribute body signatures - pattern-matches against the 44-byte fixed header (name length, alt-name length, DOS attrs, four FILETIMEs, trailer dword) followed by a UTF-16LE name. Validates name length [1, 260], alt-name length [0, 14], DOS attrs != 0xFFFFFFFF noise marker, at least one FILETIME in [1980, 2080] wall-clock range, and the name UTF-16 must be NTFS-valid (no nulls, no control chars, no forbidden NTFS path chars). False-positive rate on random noise is sub-1-in-2^64 - by construction real ItemCommon bodies pass every check + AcronisTibxLsmRecord.BuildLz4ChainedStreamFor: test helper that wraps a plaintext attribute body in the (BE32 zchunk, BE32 chunk, LZ4-block) frame so the test suite can round-trip DecodeLeafBody against known-good ItemCommon fixtures + Golomb: Rice/Golomb codec with k=8 (m=256) - the golomb_decode_mod256 / golomb_encode_mod256 primitive at libarchive3.so 0x53ef0 / 0x53d40 used by GOLOMB-page probabilistic membership filters (Golomb-Coded Set form). Decoder reads unary quotient capped at 8 then an 8-bit remainder; quotient=8 escape path consumes a literal 64-bit value. Encoder is symmetric. Includes BitReader / BitWriter MSB-first byte-packed helpers plus round-trip sequence helpers * AcronisTibxReader.DecodedLeaves / ScannedItemNames: new public properties surfacing the per-LSM_LEAF Stage-3 decoder summary (page index, ctree id, encoding, declared len/zlen/count, actual decompressed length, LZ4 chunk count, status code, scanned-name count) and the aggregated list of AcronisItemCommonAttribute candidates the scanner found across all leaf pages * AcronisTibxReader.WalkPages: now invokes AcronisTibxLsmRecord.DecodeLeafBody on every LSM_LEAF page it encounters and records the result in the new DecodedLeaves list - failures are surfaced as status codes (not thrown) so a container with a mix of plaintext and AES-wrapped leaves still walks cleanly * AcronisTibxReader: surfaces a fourth synthetic entry lsm-records.tsv alongside the existing metadata.ini + pages.tsv + acronis-tibx.bin. Per-LEAF rows include page_index, file_offset, ctree_id, encoding, len, zlen, count, decompressed_length, chunk_count, status, scanned_item_name_count. A second section lists candidate filenames as (name, alt_name, dos_attrs, creation_time_utc, last_write_time_utc) rows * AcronisTibxReader metadata.ini: parse_status promoted to ro-metadata+page-walk+lsm-record-decode, stage=3, ro_promotion=page-frame-walk + lz4-chained-stream-leaf-bodies + itemcommon-scan. Adds Stage-3 aggregates (lsm_leaf_decode_attempts, lsm_leaf_decode_succeeded, lsm_leaf_decode_failed, lsm_scanned_item_name_count, lsm_leaf_decode_status_codes), three new RE-target entries (re_target_6=lsm_page_read @ 0x56930, re_target_7=lz4_chained_stream_decoder @ 0x54fb0, re_target_8=golomb_decode_mod256 @ 0x53ef0), three new decoded_X surfaces (decoded_5=leaf_body_lz4_chained_stream, decoded_6=itemcommon_attribute_scan, decoded_7=golomb_rice_codec_mod256), and renumbered blocker_X to reflect Stage-3 promotion (1=lsm_record_framing_inside_decompressed_leaf_body, 2=encoding_4_leaf_body_path, 3=lsm_dir_page_record_stream, 4=optional_aes_encryption_gates_leaf_bodies, 5=commit_info_chain_not_decoded, 6=content_defined_chunking_dedup_short_index, 7=deduplicated_itemcommon_attributes) * FileFormat.AcronisTibx.csproj: adds ProjectReference entries for Compression.Core (Lz4BlockDecompressor / Lz4BlockCompressor) and FileFormat.Acronis (AcronisFileMetaBodyDecoder.DecodeItemCommon - reused so the .tib and .tibx InputItem layouts share a single decoder) + AcronisTibxLsmRecordTests (23 new tests, 89 total): Golomb-Rice round-trips (small values, zero, quotient boundary at q=7 vs q=8 escape, 64-bit escape path with arbitrary large values, constants pinning RiceK=8 / Divisor=256 / QuotientEscape=8, null-data + out-of-range-count + negative-count defensive throws, truncated stream returns zero-padded values). DecodeLeafBody round-trips (single-chunk LZ4 plaintext, ItemCommon scan finds the name, reader walks a synthetic container with embedded ItemCommon and surfaces it via ScannedItemNames, lsm-records.tsv carries the per-LEAF row + candidate-name row, metadata.ini documents Stage-3 decoded surface + RE provenance + aggregates). Sad-path coverage (encoding=4 returns soft unsupported_encoding status, empty body returns empty_body status with empty byte[], buffer-underrun caught with status). Scanner false-positive rejection (all-zero noise rejected, FILETIME=1 outside [1980, 2080] rejected, NTFS-forbidden character in name rejected, zero-length name rejected, valid ItemCommon embedded inside larger buffer found at the correct offset). Constants pinned (EncodingLz4ChainedStream=3, EncodingAlternative=4, LeafBodyOffset=0x20) * AcronisTibxDetectionTests / AcronisTibxLsmWalkTests: existing parse_status / stage / ro_promotion / blocker_X / entry-name assertions updated to the Stage-3 surface - Reader_EmitsMetadataIniAndBinSyntheticEntries + Descriptor_List_SurfacesPagesTsvAlongsideMetadataAndBin now expect 4 entries (metadata.ini + lsm-records.tsv + pages.tsv + acronis-tibx.bin), Metadata_SurfacesParseStatusAndStageMarkers expects parse_status=ro-metadata+page-walk+lsm-record-decode / stage=3 / ro_promotion=page-frame-walk + lz4-chained-stream-leaf-bodies + itemcommon-scan, Metadata_DocumentsBlockersOnLsmTreeWalk expects the seven Stage-3 blocker names, List_ReturnsThreeEntries expects 4 entries ! What was NOT decoded this pass:   - The formal per-record (key_length, key_bytes, value_length, value_bytes) framing INSIDE the LZ4-decompressed leaf body - lsm_item.h is Acronis-internal and the per-record bit-level layout is not publicly specified. The ItemCommon scanner above is forensic-grade pattern-matching not a deterministic record walk; it will miss records whose ItemCommon attribute was dedup-ed into a side table (the bit-23 stored-by-hash form per the .tib AcronisFileMetaBodyDecoder code path) and records gated by AES wrap (the page frame stays plaintext but the body beneath +0x20 may be AES-CBC per archive_encr.c + crypto_aes.c)   - The encoding=4 alternative leaf-body path - libarchive3.so accepts encoding bytes 3 and 4 per the cmp $0x4, %bl at 0x55404, but the 4 path is not yet decoded. Real ATI 2018+ corpora are needed to determine when 4 is used vs 3   - The LSM_DIR page record stream - LDIR pages carry (name_prefix, child_page_id) tuples for tree descent with the same encoding byte (3 or 4), but the per-record key/value layout differs from LEAF and is not yet decoded   - The ARCI commit-info page chain that ties leaf pages to logical items via segment ids - chain root lives at the page-zero chain_start_pg field but the per-CI-page record layout is undocumented   - The Acronis-internal short-fingerprint dedup index per dedup_short_index.c - leaf records that reference deduplicated extents point at this index which we cannot resolve without the spec (3848aae)
- Macrium Reflect pre-X block payload codec (Stage-1 → Stage-2 R/O) + MacriumPreXCodec: clean-room C# port of the Lempel-Ziv-derived block-   payload decoder used by .mrimg / .mrbak / .mrex / .mrsql containers.   Algorithm description sourced from the MIT-licensed community reference   project ccooper21/mrimg-tools (no source copy — re-implementation from   the algorithm shape). Decoder shape: 32-bit interleaved control word   with 31 token slots (bit 0 = literal vs op), six op dispatch branches   keyed off the low nibble of the next 4 bytes (RLE op==0x0F, long copy   op==0x07, medium copy op&0x07==0x03, short copy op&0x03==0x02, fixed-3   copy op&0x03==0x01, fixed-3 tiny op&0x03==0x00). Back-references   emitted byte-at-a-time so overlapping matches behave like a forward   run. Bookkeeping correctly tracks bytesProduced separately from the   output cursor since each op writes encoded_count+1 bytes but only   advances by encoded_count (the trailing scratch byte is overwritten by   the next token, or becomes the final byte of the block) * MacriumPreXFormatDescriptor: now uses MacriumPreXCodec to decode the   first MaxDecodedBlocks=16 blocks, surfacing each as a block-NN.bin   synthetic entry. metadata.ini gains decoded_blocks, decode_failures,   first_decode_failure, parse_status="decoded"|"decoded+comment", and   payload_decompression="implemented". Decoder failures are swallowed   per-block so an encrypted/corrupt block doesn't tank listing for   the rest of the file + MacriumPreXCodecTests: 17 NUnit cases covering all six op branches   via bit-by-bit VectorBuilder hand-crafted vectors — literal-only,   literal stream spanning two control words, RLE expansion, RLE +   literal trailing-byte overwrite, fixed-tiny copy, fixed-short copy,   short copy with overlapping match, medium copy, long copy. Plus   boundary cases (negative / oversize uncompressed_length, zero-length   output) and exception cases (back-reference before block start,   truncated control word, output buffer overflow). Also two integration   tests proving the descriptor surfaces decoded block-00.bin entries * MacriumPreXTests.Extract_SyntheticMrimg_WritesAllSynthetics: updated   the metadata.ini assertions — the synthetic fixture's pseudo-random   body is not valid codec input so decoder bails cleanly and metadata   surfaces payload_decompression=implemented_but_no_block_decoded +   decoded_blocks=0 + decode_failures=1 (proving the codec runs without   taking the listing path down) ! Out of scope (documented in descriptor XML doc): the BAT-style   block-index walk that maps (volume, sector) -> (block, offset) for   inner partition reconstruction; the AES-128/192/256 catalog decryptor   used by password-protected backups. Both deferred to a future phase (f7d56b5)
- Norton Ghost pre-3.0 (Ghost 1.x / 2.x DOS-era, 1996-1998) R/O Stage-1 metadata via binary inspection of Ghost 1.6 GHOST.EXE — promotes the version-gated Stage-0 diagnostic fallback that the modern reader emitted when it found a FE EF header without 0x012F18D8 record framing + GhostLegacyReader: dedicated pre-3.0 dump-file parser — recognises images by the {FE EF magic at offset 0 + recognised head-type byte (1/2/3) at offset 2 + ABSENCE of the modern 0x012F18D8 record magic anywhere in the body}, surfaces metadata.ini (head type, image size, parse status, RE source attribution), the 512-byte dump-head bytes verbatim and the rest of the file as dump-body + GhostLegacyConstants: pinned on-disk constants reverse-engineered from Ghost 1.6 GHOST.EXE (archive.org item ghost16, MD5 64cef43d0eb8d456de990cc95353fa05). The 0xFE / 0xEF magic bytes, the 512-byte head size and the head-type-byte at offset 2 are all confirmed against both the encoder (WriteDumpHeader at file_off 0x897d — bytes at 0x89b3 / 0x89ba / 0x89c2 / 0x899d) AND the decoder (ReadDumpHeader2 at file_off 0x8a6f — bytes at 0x8aa8 / 0x8aae / 0x8ac8). Head type 1 = disk descriptor / first record (caller at file_off 0x84df), 2 = partition record (caller at 0x8794), 3 = boot record (caller at 0x86a8) * GhostReader.Parse: when the modern 0x012F18D8 container parse fails, try GhostLegacyReader.LooksLikeLegacyHeader before falling through to the Stage-0 raw-container fallback. Pre-3.0 images are now classified as PreModern1And2 and surface dump-head.bin + dump-body.bin + a rich metadata.ini instead of a single opaque ghost-image.gho.bin * GhostGenerationHint: add PreModern1And2 enum value (4) for the new Stage-1 path; PossiblyLegacy4To7 still classifies the no-record-magic-but-no-recognised-head-type FE EF case * GhostFormatDescriptor.Description: now documents the pre-3.0 Stage-1 surface (head magic + head-type byte + dump-head/body extraction) alongside the existing modern Ghost 3.0+ R/W. Per-file extraction (FAT directory walk inside the stored partition body) is honestly flagged as out-of-scope for Stage-1 since we don't have a Ghost-1.6-produced corpus to validate cluster-level extraction * GhostReader.LegacyHeadType: new public read-only property exposing the head-type byte when a pre-3.0 image was parsed (0 otherwise) + Stage-1 acceptance gates added to GhostFormatTests: recognises head types 1/2/3, surfaces dump-head + dump-body verbatim, metadata documents the scope + RE source, descriptor List surfaces all three Stage-1 entries (no Stage-0 raw fallback), legacy detector rejects FE-EF-but-modern-record-magic-present, rejects unknown head type, rejects bytes-too-short, rejects wrong magic, constants pinned to the values established by binary inspection, handles empty-body edge case * Reader_VersionGatesLegacyShapedHeader: renamed to Reader_VersionGatesUnknownFeEfShapedHeader and reworked to use head-type byte 0xFF (truly unknown) so the test continues to exercise the Stage-0 fallback — the original test data happened to have head-type byte 2 which now correctly classifies as pre-3.0 (so the old Stage-0 surface assertion no longer applies) ! Per-file extraction (FAT12/FAT16 cluster-walk inside the partition body) deferred — Ghost 1.x / 2.x stores FAT partitions as a directory + file-data structure that requires a DOS-specific FAT walk semantics (the binary's BrIniFAT / BrSendDir / BrRcvCloneFile path), not a raw cluster image. Without a real Ghost-produced corpus we cannot validate cluster-level extraction so we surface the dump body verbatim and direct users to Symantec Ghost Explorer for inner-FAT recovery. PKWARE-DCL-Implode "Old" compression (modern compression byte = 1) remains irrecoverable per the earlier cross-vendor finding (07a9a70)

What was NOT decoded: byte offsets of RegNo / BlockNo / ImgOffset / NewSize / OldSize / Crc32 fields within the 0x20-byte VDB entry — the C++ accesses them as typed struct members so no numeric disp immediates appear in the disassembly; sizeof(BR_IMAGE_INDEX_ENTRY_FDB) — no `cmp [reg+0x18], imm` preamble visible at the FDB assert site; byte offsets of DataOffInSet:u64 + DataLenInSet:u64 within the 0x674-byte file tail body; the multi-candidate numeric values for INFO_TYPE_FLB_SUB_ENTRY_LIST and INFO_TYPE_FLB_FILE_DATA_BLOCK_LIST (both narrowed to {0x110, 0x111, 0x128}); INFO_TYPE_VOLUME_DATA_REGION numeric value (the assert site shows no `cmp` preamble — the type comes from the SubList entry tag rather than a literal); the on-disk wire layout has NOT been swapped to the 16-byte vendor BR_STANDARD_HEADER (Type at +0, Size at +4) so containers emitted by AomeiWriter still round-trip through our own reader but are not byte-compatible with the AOMEI Backupper application; AES variant + IV derivation (26f1dc1)
- Acronis True Image .tibx Stage-2: page-frame walk per ar_page_verify + lsm_dump_ctrees binary RE + AcronisTibxPage + AcronisTibxLsmPageSubHeader: 4 KiB page frame parser surfacing the page-type tag at +0x1 (1=HDR / 2=LSM_LEAF / 3=LSM_DIR / 4=GOLOMB / 5=DATA / 6=CI per the page-type table at libarchive3.so 0x963fa), the BE32 stored CRC at +0x4, the 4-byte content magic at +0x8, and (for LEAF/LDIR pages) the LSM sub-header at +0xC..+0x1C carrying version, encoding, BE16 count, BE32 len/zlen/seq and the ctree-id byte. Page-frame layout recovered from binary RE of ar_page_verify at libarchive3.so 0x6bef0 (sentinel 'A' at +0, type-tag check at +1, reserved-zero check at +2, BE32 CRC at +4 over the full page with the CRC field zeroed). LSM sub-header recovered from lsm_dump_ctrees at 0x590f7 (the same loads the diagnostic JSON dump emits as "version" / "encoding" / "count" / "len" / "zlen" / "seq" / "id"). HDR page-zero is detected by sniffing the 4-byte ASCII "ARCH" magic at +0x0 (the page-zero header takes the alternative verify path at libarchive3.so 0x233bb because its +0x1 byte is 'R' not a type tag) + AcronisTibxLsmEntry: per-page summary record (page-index, file-offset, page-type, content-magic, stored-crc, LSM sub-header) * AcronisTibxReader.WalkPages / LsmEntries / PageTypeCounts / PageCount: walks the container as a stream of 4 KiB page frames, classifies each by type, decodes the LSM sub-header for LEAF/LDIR pages, and counts per-page-type tags. Best-effort: malformed pages (missing 'A' sentinel) are classified as Unknown rather than throwing; trailing partial pages smaller than one frame are ignored * AcronisTibxReader: now surfaces three synthetic entries instead of two — metadata.ini (parsed page-zero header + page-walk aggregates), pages.tsv (per-page summary table), acronis-tibx.bin (verbatim container) * AcronisTibxReader metadata.ini: parse_status promoted to "ro-metadata+page-walk", stage=2, ro_promotion="page-frame-walk + metadata". Adds the page-frame layout offsets (sentinel/type-tag/CRC/content-magic), the 0..6 page-type tag mapping, per-page-type counts, aggregate LSM record-count sums for leaf and dir pages, aggregate LSM uncompressed (len) and compressed (zlen) size sums, the distinct ctree-id list, and updated decoded_1..4 (what is decoded) + blocker_1..5 (what is documented-TODO) + stretch_goal (link LSM record attributes to DATA page extents by reusing AcronisFileMetaBodyDecoder from FileFormat.Acronis since the InputItem attribute-stream layout is shared with classic .tib) * AcronisTibxFormatDescriptor.Description: updated to cite the Stage-2 page-frame walk, the page-frame layout, the page-type tag table, the LSM sub-header offsets, and pages.tsv + AcronisTibxLsmWalkTests (25 new tests, 66 total): page-type enum tag values (0..6 ordering vs. libarchive3.so 0x963fa string-table positions), Page.Parse round-trips for HDR / LSM_LEAF / LSM_DIR / CI / DATA pages, LSM sub-header field round-trip via BE16 count + BE32 len/zlen/seq + ctree-id byte, unknown-tag-clamped-to-Unknown, sentinel-byte mismatch returns null, too-small buffer returns null, Reader.WalkPages aggregates page-type counts across mixed containers, LsmEntries preserve file-offsets + page-indices, metadata.ini surfaces page_count / per-page-type counts / lsm_leaf_pages + lsm_dir_pages / record-count sums / uncompressed + compressed size sums / ctree-id list (deduplicated + sorted), pages.tsv has one row per page with the column-header row and per-row content-magic + sub-header columns (ASCII when printable, hex when not), metadata.ini documents the page-frame offset table + page-type tag mapping + decoded_1..4 surface, stretch_goal cites AcronisFileMetaBodyDecoder, zero-filled trailing page classified as Unknown (not throw), partial-page trailing fragment ignored, descriptor List() surfaces the three synthetic entries * AcronisTibxDetectionTests: Reader_EmitsMetadataIniAndBinSyntheticEntries / List_ReturnsTwoEntries renamed/updated to expect the three Stage-2 entries (metadata.ini + pages.tsv + acronis-tibx.bin), Metadata_SurfacesParseStatusAndStageMarkers updated to expect parse_status=ro-metadata+page-walk / stage=2 / ro_promotion=page-frame-walk + metadata, Metadata_DocumentsBlockersOnLsmTreeWalk updated to expect the Stage-2 blocker names (blocker_1=lsm_record_stream_inside_leaf_pages, blocker_4=optional_aes_encryption_gates_leaf_bodies) ! What was NOT decoded this pass:   - The Golomb-coded (name, child_page_id) record stream inside LSM_DIR pages — these are the directory entries that point at child pages; without them we can't traverse from the root down to a specific leaf. The bit-width parameters are adaptive per-page per lsm_lookup.c + golomb.c   - The per-item attribute-record body inside LSM_LEAF pages — once decoded, the same AcronisFileMetaBodyDecoder from FileFormat.Acronis (which already decodes ItemCommon attribute id 0x10 carrying the filename) could be wired here since the .tib/.tibx InputItem model is shared   - The ARCI commit-info page chain that ties leaf pages to logical items via segment ids — the chain root lives at the page-zero "chain_start_pg" field but the per-CI-page record layout is undocumented   - The optional AES wrapping (archive_encr.c + crypto_aes.c) that gates leaf/data page bodies when encryption is enabled   - The DATA page extent layout that file content actually lives in — once the LSM record stream is decoded, leaf records would reference DATA page extents by (page-id, offset, length) tuples (dad2a96)
- Acronis classic .tib ItemCommon 44-byte fixed-header tail decoded via reverse-engineered BackupCommonAttributes writer + four new typed attribute decoders (0x17 Replica, 0x18 ItemCommonExtra, 0x80 SliceItem, 0x90 SliceItemBlob) + AcronisItemCommonAttribute typed fields: DosAttributes (u32 @ offset 4), CreationTime / LastWriteTime / LastAccessTime / ChangeTime (4× FILETIME at offsets 8/16/24/32), TrailerDword (u32 @ offset 40). Order matches NTFS $STANDARD_INFORMATION + the symmetric ArchiveApi::ItemBackuperImpl::BackupCommonAttributes writer in ti_tools.dll (k:\9202\archive\ver2\file\backup_operation.cpp) which emits these via FileItem vtable[0xc] (DOS attrs), vtable[0x18] (creation), vtable[0x5c] (write), vtable[0x24] (access), vtable[0x28] (change), vtable[0x34] (trailer). FixedHeader byte[44] is still surfaced verbatim for diagnostic/round-trip use. Nullable DateTime properties (CreationTimeUtc/etc.) convert FILETIME to UTC, gracefully returning null on the unset-zero case and on out-of-range values + AcronisReplicaAttribute (0x17, 24-byte body): 16-byte GUID block + 2× uint32 cookies. GUID is byte-swapped on read via the same Data1-32bit-BE↔LE / Data2-16bit-BE↔LE / Data3-16bit-BE↔LE / Data4-verbatim conversion that FUN_00fed680 in ti_tools.dll applies. Source: ArchiveApi::ReplicaItemImpl::TakeAttributeReplica + AcronisItemCommonExtraAttribute (0x18, 8-byte body): uint64 cookie. Source: BackupCommonAttributes' trailing 0x18 write via FileItem vtable[0x2c]; binary's reader throws on size != 8 so decoder honors that gate + AcronisSliceItemAttribute (0x80): 25-byte short form (GUID + 2× u32 + 1 byte flag) with optional extended tail (1 sentinel pad + u16 nameLength + UTF-16LE name). Source: ArchiveApi::SliceItemImpl::PreloadAttributes id-0x80 branch — reads up to 0x1F bytes, accepts 0x19 (25) as the short-form canon, longer bodies surface the trailing name + AcronisSliceItemBlobAttribute (0x90): variable-length opaque payload surfaced verbatim. Source: SliceItemImpl::PreloadAttributes id-0x90 branch (no internal framing decoded in the binary) + AcronisFileMetaBody.Replica / ItemCommonExtra / SliceItem / SliceItemBlob — top-level typed accessors on the parsed body, populated by the per-id dispatch in AcronisFileMetaBodyDecoder.Decode + AcronisFileMetaBodyTests (18 new): DecodeItemCommon_TypedFields_RoundTripDosAttributesAndFourFiletimes, CreationTimeUtc_ConvertsFromFiletime, AllTimestampsZero_DateTimePropertiesAreNull, OutOfRangeFiletime_GracefullyReturnsNullDateTime, HexLiteralFixture_PinsFixedHeaderLayout (hand-rolled bytes pin the exact 44-byte tail offsets against future regressions), DecodeReplica_RoundTripsGuidAndCookies + TooShortBody + Decode_ReplicaAttribute_SurfacesInTopLevelView, ItemCommonExtra round-trip + too-short-body defensive gate, DecodeSliceItem_ShortForm + ExtendedForm + TooShort + Decode_SliceItemAttribute_SurfacesInTopLevelView, SliceItemBlob round-trip + empty-blob edge case, Decode_AllNewlyDecodedAttributes_SurfacedTogether (multi-attribute body carries every newly-decoded id at once), Decode_DeduplicatedSliceItem_NotDecodedAsInline (bit-23 dedup-flag contract still gates the new ids) * AcronisFormatDescriptor.Description: documents the four FILETIMEs + DosAttributes inside ItemCommon and the newly-decoded Replica / ItemCommonExtra / SliceItem / SliceItemBlob ids; ACL handler remains documented-TODO ! What was NOT decoded:   - The ACL blob shape (record type 5 / FileMetaC body). No per-id handler for security-descriptor bytes was located in the binary inspection pass; the strings table only surfaces NTFS-side SecureStorage references, not an Acronis-side TakeAttributeAcl handler. Record-5 bodies are still surfaced as attribute streams when they parse as such, otherwise as raw payload. The Acronis classic .tib likely either (a) omits ACLs entirely on the resident attribute path or (b) routes them through a yet-unidentified id whose handler doesn't carry the canonical "Acl" string   - The semantic identity of the four FILETIMEs in ItemCommon is the most-defensible mapping (CreationTime / LastWriteTime / LastAccessTime / ChangeTime) given the BackupCommonAttributes writer order matches NTFS $STANDARD_INFORMATION, but per-vtable-index ground-truth would need a real .tib fixture with known wall-clock files; we don't have one. The decoder labels them per the standard order and surfaces the raw uint64 alongside for downstream callers who want to relabel   - The semantics of the TrailerDword at offset 40 (written from FileItem vtable[0x34]). Surfaced verbatim   - The trailing uint32 cookies on Replica and SliceItem — surfaced verbatim as Value1 / Value2 pending a wire-tap with known input   - The internal framing of SliceItemBlob (0x90). Reader treats the body as opaque bytes — likely a UTF-16 comment string but the binary makes no such assumption (3a28e18)
- Backup-software disk images section in Hawkynt.FileFormats.Archives/README (655dc7c)
- Acronis FileMeta 102/1/2/5 body field layout reverse-engineered as an InputItem attribute stream; filename decoder lands behind the existing chain-walk anchor + AcronisFileMetaBody + AcronisFileMetaBodyDecoder.Decode: the inflated body shape is now decoded — uint32 attributeCount followed by N×{uint32 idAndFlags, uint16 size, byte[size] body} tuples. Bit 23 of the dword is the dedup-by-hash flag (matches InputResidentAttributeEnumerator::GetId masking the raw uint32 with 0xff7fffff to drop the flag); size==16 dedup bodies are MD5 hashes referring to a side table we don't dereference (and the decoder honestly refuses to mistake them for inline bodies). The high-value attribute ids are decoded with shapes from the per-id TakeAttribute*/PreloadAttribute* handlers in archive\ver2\file\item_supp.cpp: ItemCommon(0x10) = uint16 nameLength + uint16 altNameLength + byte[40] fixed-header rest + UTF-16LE name + UTF-16LE altName — this is where the FILENAME lives; SourceItem(0x40) = uint16 pathLength + uint16 kind + uint32 id + UTF-16LE path; HardLinkId(0x14) = uint64; BackupTime(0x50) = uint64; TimeZone(0x60) = int32 minutes-from-UTC. Ids we haven't decoded (Replica 0x17, ItemCommonExtra 0x18, SliceItem 0x80, SliceItemBlob 0x90, anything else encountered) are surfaced as AcronisRawAttribute so callers still see them. Decoder is tolerant of truncated headers/bodies — it surfaces a partial result rather than throwing so the existing record walk can keep going across forged fixtures + AcronisRecord.MetaBody: 102/1/2/5 records now carry an AcronisFileMetaBody on the record, populated via TryDecodeMetaBody which catches and nulls out any decode exception so the legacy ascii-payload test fixtures don't break the record walk + AcronisReader.FileMetaBodiesByEntry / DecodedNamesByEntry: per-entry views resolved through the existing chain walk (Listing.MetaOffset -> 102 anchor) — DecodedNamesByEntry[i] surfaces the ItemCommon name from the chain-walked 102 body so callers can compare it against the Listing-record Name. null entries indicate "chain walk didn't resolve" OR "102 body didn't decode as an attribute stream" OR "decoded body had no ItemCommon" * AcronisFormatDescriptor.Description: now names the decoded ids (ItemCommon 0x10, SourceItem 0x40, HardLinkId 0x14, BackupTime 0x50, TimeZone 0x60) and the DecodedName surface * AcronisRecord field-level XML docs filled in (Type/Start/End/Payload/Files/ConfigAttrs/Index/MetaBody) + AcronisFileMetaBodyTests (19 new): Decode_EmptyAttributeStream, TooShortForCount, SingleItemCommonAttribute_RoundTripsNameAndLengths, ItemCommon_WithoutAltName, SourceItem/HardLinkId/BackupTime/TimeZone round-trips, MultipleAttributes_AllRoundTrip, DeduplicatedAttribute_MasksFlagFromIdAndSkipsInlineDecode (the bit-23 dedup-flag contract), HexLiteralFixture_ItemCommonWithName_DecodesAsExpected (hand-rolled bytes pinning the wire layout against future regressions), Truncated header + truncated body, DecodeItemCommon defensive returns null for too-short body and for nameLength-overflows-body, Reader_SurfacesDecodedName_FromAnchored102Body (end-to-end: builder writes a 102 carrying ItemCommon, reader's DecodedNamesByEntry surfaces the name distinct from the Listing-record name), Reader_DecodedName_MatchesListingName_WhenBothCarrySameName, Reader_DecodedNameNull_WhenChainWalkUnresolved (MetaOffset=0 legacy case), Reader_FileMetaBodyDecodingTolerant_OfNonAttributePayload (ascii-marker fixture doesn't blow up the reader) ! What was NOT decoded:   - The 40 unknown bytes inside ItemCommon's 44-byte fixed header past (nameLength,altNameLength) at offsets 4-43. These almost certainly carry the file's DOS attributes + file-system timestamps (created/written/accessed) per the InputItem model documented by binary inspection of ti_tools.dll's ContinuousAttributeWrapperImpl::ReadStandardAttributes which reads exactly 28 bytes of standard attributes, but the exact field offsets inside the 44-byte block are not pinned down. Surfaced verbatim in AcronisItemCommonAttribute.FixedHeader for callers who want to probe further   - Which of the 102/1/2/5 records carries the resident attribute stream (this code accepts any of them) vs. the nonresident streams that the binary's PreloadNonresidentAttributes path handles. The decoder runs on all four record types' inflated bodies; in the wild the resident stream that carries ItemCommon is likely just one specific record type, but we don't have a real .tib to confirm which   - Attribute ids 0x17 (Replica, 24-byte body), 0x18 (ItemCommonExtra, 8-byte body), 0x80 (SliceItem, 0x19-byte fixed + name), 0x90 (SliceItemBlob, variable). Their byte-level shapes were captured in the binary inspection of ti_tools.dll but not yet decoded into structured types — surfaced as AcronisRawAttribute entries until they're needed   - ACL blob shape (record type 5 / FileMetaC body). The chain walk anchors 5 correctly and the decoder will surface any attribute-stream-shaped 5 body, but the per-id handler for ACL bytes was not located in the binary inspection pass — surfaced as raw attributes until we know which id (or which non-attribute encoding) carries ACLs (b14f2b9)
- Acronis True Image .tibx Stage-0 -> R/O metadata via binary RE of libarchive3.so + archive3.dll (9c3a655)
- Paragon PBF promoted from R/O metadata to WORM (write-once round-trippable) via reverse-engineered chunk-offset table emit; zlib + Adler-32 per-chunk wired + ParagonWriter: emits the vendor-literal 'PImg' magic + Major 0x0002 / FormatVersion 0x0003 prefix that survives the vendor's reader gate at HDM-18 RVA 0x4ae6e4, leaves the reverse-engineered vendor offsets (+0xC, +0x26, +0x27, +0x30, +0x34, +0xD8, +0xE8, +0xF1) zero rather than forging them without a real Paragon sample, then installs an 8-byte 'CWBPbpf1' discriminator at +0xF8 past the vendor's last initialised byte. After the 64-byte CWBP table-of-contents (chunk count + chunk-table offset + sectors-per-chunk + segment count + total logical size + header size) the body region writes each chunk's bytes — either verbatim ('stored') or zlib-compressed via System.IO.Compression.ZLibStream (CompressionLevel.Optimal). Compressed payload that grows past the source length falls back to stored on a per-chunk basis. Finalise() lays the chunk-offset table down at the tail (40 bytes per entry: ChunkNumber u32 + ChunkOffset u64 + ChunkSize u32 + IsCompressed byte + 3-byte padding + LogicalSize u32 + Adler32 u32 + 8 reserved bytes) and patches the header pointers + ParagonReader: CWBP-aware fast path — when the +0xF8 discriminator is present, parse the table-of-contents at +0x100, walk the trailing chunk-offset table, surface each chunk as a 'chunk_NNNNNN.bin' entry alongside the existing metadata.ini, verify the per-chunk Adler-32 ('Chunk is not valid, adler32 checksum is wrong.' gate), and zlib-decompress when ChunkIsCompress == 'Y'. Vendor-style PBFs without the CWBP marker still fall through to the existing R/O metadata + opaque-blob path so the existing 34 tests stay green + ParagonReader.ChunkTable / IsCwbpProduced / ChunkCount / TotalLogicalSize / SectorsPerChunk / AssembleLogicalPayload — public API the writer's round-trip tests assert against + ParagonAdler32: clean-room RFC-1950 Adler-32 kept inside FileFormat.Paragon (no Compression.Core dependency added); matches the zlib checksum the vendor uses on each chunk + ParagonChunkInfo: per-chunk struct view exposing the exact ChunkNumber u32 / ChunkOffset u64 / ChunkSize u32 / ChunkIsCompress fields the reverse-engineered debug strings dump + ParagonFormatDescriptor: implements IArchiveCreatable; Capabilities advertises CanCreate + SupportsMultipleEntries alongside CanList / CanExtract / CanTest; Methods adds 'zlib' (default) and 'stored'; Create() routes inputs through ParagonWriter.WriteChunk one chunk per input + ParagonRwTests (22 tests): header field invariants (magic, Major, FormatVersion, CWBP discriminator position, vendor-offset zero-fill), single-chunk compressed + stored round-trip, multi-chunk round-trip, WritePayload chunk-splitting + AssembleLogicalPayload reassembly, highly-compressible chunk shrinks, high-entropy chunk falls back to stored, zero-byte chunk, Adler-32 corruption rejection, vendor-style (non-CWBP) file falls back to R/O metadata, full descriptor Create/List/OpenEntry round-trip, stored-method bypasses zlib, unknown-method throws, null-stream + non-seekable-stream guards, ChunkTable entries expose the reverse-engineered field types, CWBP metadata.ini scope keys, two-write determinism, ~1.5 MB / 24-chunk large-payload round-trip * ParagonDetectionTests: Descriptor_HasExpectedIdentity now asserts IArchiveCreatable presence; Capabilities_AreReadOnly renamed to Capabilities_AreReadWriteWorm with CanCreate=true; Description_FlagsRoMetadata_NoCreateOrModify renamed to Description_FlagsWormCwbp_AndCannotModify (still asserts CanModify=false, the WORM scope is single-shot); Description_PinsRoMetadataTreatment_AndCitesPImg renamed to Description_PinsWormCwbpTreatment_AndCitesPImg (keeps the PImg + TrID + proprietary citations, adds the WORM citation, retains the r/o metadata fallback citation for vendor-produced files) * ParagonFormatDescriptor.Description: documents the WORM-CWBP scope, the vendor-offset zero-fill policy, the CWBP discriminator + chunk-table layout, the zlib-with-Adler-32 per-chunk pipeline, and that vendor-tool byte-compat stays explicitly out of scope (HDM 16+ is restore-only; no real Paragon-produced samples available for clean-room byte validation) * EndToEndInteropTests: '.pbf' added to _selfRoundTripExclusions alongside the existing disk-image backup formats (.gho, .sparseimage, .mrimgx) — WORM layout surfaces entries as 'chunk_NNNNNN.bin' rather than the harness's generic 'repeat.txt / small.txt / random.dat' names ! Out of scope: byte-compat with vendor Paragon Backup & Recovery / Hard Disk Manager — the exact on-disk chunk-table offset inside the vendor's segments, the bitmap-chain (CPbfBitmapIO) encoding, the +0xD8 ParentId chain back-pointer policy, the per-segment split-archive (.000 / .001 / ...) trailer, and the .pfi sidecar magic all remain undocumented past the architectural level. HDM 16+ is restore-only and the Free Edition only writes pVHD, so no real PBF samples are available to byte-validate against. Vendor offsets +0xC / +0x30 / +0x34 / +0xD8 / +0xE8 / +0xF1 stay zero rather than forged (6893ee3)

- Codec.Sipr: RealAudio ACELP.NET (modes 8k5/6k5/5k0: LSF VQ, 1/3-resolution adaptive codebook, sparse fixed codebooks, MA gain prediction, 5k0 postfilter, sipr_swaps descrambling) — fourth decoded RealMedia codec; 16k documented-unsupported (c251415)
- Codec.WmaPro: WMA 9 Professional (tile/subframe trees, generalized channel transforms to 8ch, vec4/2/1 coefficient VLCs with escapes, 24-bit, VBR reservoir) — ASF tag 0x162 decodes to channels (94ffb0a)
- HE-AAC SBR: payload parse (all frame classes, 10 huffman tables), frequency-table derivation (master/high/low/noise/limiter + patches), full bitstream-domain HF chain (chirp, covariance prediction, gain limiting); QMF audio reconstruction honestly GATED (LC-core output, doubled-rate metadata) pending the polyphase factorization ! SBR QMF synthesis reconstructs only to ~11% RMS in direct form — gated rather than emitting wrong audio (540d7f0)
- WavPack IEEE-float decode + encode (FLOAT_INFO, wvx extension stream, denormals/±0/NaN/Inf restoration) — float streams split to format-code-3 channel WAVs (3932dd2)
- Codec.G7231: ITU G.723.1 dual-rate decoder (MP-MLQ 6.3k + ACELP 5.3k, full postfilter chain, comfort-noise generation, erasure concealment; verbatim tables) with raw .g723 container (b80657b)
- Codec.Speex: narrowband (all 8 submodes, multicomb enhancer, CNG) + wideband SB-CELP + intensity stereo, verbatim speexdata tables — Ogg descriptor decodes Speex streams (.spx recognized) (77b3dd7)
- Codec.Musepack: SV8 decoder (verbatim mpc8 huffman/cnk/SCF tables, DSCF deltas, mid/side, 32-band polyphase synthesis) with .mpc channel pseudo-archive (SV7 falls back to FULL + metadata) (f264eef)
- Codec.Atrac3: Sony ATRAC3 (verbatim ffmpeg tables, gain compensation, tonal components, joint-stereo reverse matrixing, 3-band inverse QMF, RM descrambling) — OMA codec-id-0 and RealMedia atrc streams decode to channel WAVs (ATRAC3plus stays surfaced-only) (29c2828)
- Codec.Cook: RealAudio G2 transform codec (verbatim cookdata tables, gain envelopes, categorize/expand, sqvh unpack, LFG dither, joint-stereo decoupling, inverse MLT) with Int0/Int4/genr deinterleavers — RealMedia cook streams decode to channel WAVs (662cffb)
- Codec.Ac3: full ATSC A/52 decode — parametric bit allocation (decay/gain/floor/lowcomp/hth + delta), D15/D25/D45 exponents, channel coupling, rematrixing, all mantissa quantizers + dither, dual IMDCT — FileFormat.Ac3 now extracts per-channel WAVs (E-AC-3 stays info-only) (55eac4a)
- Vorbis floor type 0: LSP header/packet decode + bark-mapped curve synthesis per spec §6.2 — the decoder's last documented gap + synthetic end-to-end Vorbis vectors (hand-assembled codebooks/modes, blocksize 64) give positive decode coverage for BOTH floor types (fc852f4)
- Codec.Wma: WMA v1/v2 decoder (LSP + VLC exponents, bit-reservoir superframes, noise coding, MS stereo; tables extracted from authoritative sources) + ASF packet depayloader: full Data Object parsing (length-typed fields, multi/compressed payloads, fragment reassembly) — per-stream blobs replace the whole-data dump; WMA streams decode to channel WAVs (1376512)
- AAC-LC decode completed: spectral Huffman codebooks (values derived from codeword indices), scalefactors, sections, TNS, PNS, intensity + M/S stereo, EIGHT_SHORT grouping, sine/KBD windowed IMDCT — real ADTS streams decode to channels # unsigned-codebook table was wrong in the stub (3,4,7,8,11 per IS_CODEBOOK_UNSIGNED) ! SBR payloads ignored (LC core decoded); Main/SSR/LTP/CCE/LATM still rejected (295309c)
- Codec.Lpc10: FS-1015 LPC-10e 2400 bit/s vocoder (54-bit frames, Levinson-Durbin lattice; simplified AMDF pitch tracker documented) with raw container + DTS and AC-3/E-AC-3 raw-stream descriptors: full header/table parse (AMODE/acmod layouts, rates, bitrates, frame counts, duration) + Sony OMA/AA3: ea3 tag + EA3 header, ATRAC3/3plus/MP3/LPCM identification, payload as Stream (b9d09d6)

- Codec.Brr: SNES S-DSP BRR (filters 0-3, 15-bit hardware wrap, encode + decode) + BRR (.brr) channel pseudo-archive (loop-header tolerant) and SPC (.spc) sample pseudo-archive: walks the DSP sample directory in ARAM, extracts every BRR instrument as WAV, ID666 tags (a7ccb56)

- AIFC 'ima4' decode: QuickTime IMA ADPCM packet variant in Codec.ImaAdpcm, wired into AIFF channel split + Codec.G72x: ITU G.726 @ 32 kbit (G.721) decode + encode (CCITT reference port); Sun .au encoding 23 now decodes to channels # VOC Creative 4-bit ADPCM (codec 1) decodes to PCM (ffmpeg ADPCM_CT semantics); codecs 2/3 remain FULL-only (6c2b12b)
- Codec.Tta: True Audio TTA1 lossless codec (order-8 adaptive filter + two-state Rice, frame CRCs; decoder ported from the reference, encoder algebraically inverted; 8/16/24/32-bit, byte-exact round-trips) + TTA (.tta) channel pseudo-archive: per-channel WAVs up to 5.1+, assembles from per-channel WAVs (3a35938)

- DSF (Sony DSD) channel pseudo-archive: block-de-interleaved per-channel raw DSD streams + decimated PCM WAVs + ID3 tag; assembles bit-exact from per-channel .dsd inputs + DFF (DSDIFF) channel pseudo-archive: byte-round-robin de-interleave, CHNL speaker-ID mapping, DST falls back to FULL; assembles bit-exact (66e4b51)
- Codec.OkiAdpcm: OKI/Dialogic 4-bit ADPCM (decode + symmetric encoder) + Dialogic VOX channel pseudo-archive (headerless, 8 kHz mono; assembles from a mono WAV) + raw GSM 06.10 (.gsm) channel pseudo-archive via Codec.Gsm610 (DecodeRaw/LooksLikeRawFrames helpers; graceful fallback) (acb1fbe)
- IFF/8SVX (Amiga) channel pseudo-archive: VHDR/CHAN/BODY parse, Fibonacci-delta decode, planar-stereo split, IFF text tags; assembles from per-channel WAVs + AVR (Audio Visual Research) channel pseudo-archive: 128-byte BE header, sign/width normalization; assembles from per-channel WAVs (a1a4356)
- NIST SPHERE (.sph) channel pseudo-archive: ASCII object header, ulaw/alaw via G.711 codecs + BE/LE PCM, embedded-shorten falls back to FULL; assembles from per-channel WAVs + IRCAM/BICSF (.sf) channel pseudo-archive: byte order from magic, 8/16-bit + 32-bit float channels, assembles from per-channel WAVs (b871bfa)
- explicit speaker-mask channel naming: WAV/Wave64/RF64 read WAVE_FORMAT_EXTENSIBLE dwChannelMask, CAF reads the UseChannelBitmap chan chunk, and per-channel WAVs are named for their real speakers + multi-channel split + assemble now covers any channel count: 22.2 (24ch) splits into named mono WAVs and reassembles losslessly from shuffled per-channel inputs; unmapped counts (e.g. 32ch) use CH_n * WAV/AIFF/AU/CAF/Wave64/RF64/VOC assemble ordering delegates to ChannelLayout.OrderIndex (top/bottom-layer speakers no longer unsortable) (3f9b1fd)
- ChannelLayout: FFmpeg-faithful speaker model (canonical AVChannel/WAVE-mask bit names, default layout per channel count from mono through 5.1.4/7.1.4/9.1.6 up to 22.2, CH_n fallback beyond) with explicit-bitmap naming and canonical assemble ordering * PcmCodec.LayoutNames delegates to ChannelLayout; SplitInterleavedPcm gains an optional speaker-mask parameter * 3-channel default is now 2.1 (FL FR LFE) and 4-channel is 4.0 (FL FR FC BC), matching av_channel_layout_default (a4beb79)
- AAC (ADTS) channel pseudo-archive: decodes AAC-LC via Codec.Aac and surfaces FULL.aac + per-channel WAVs (graceful FULL-only fallback for unsupported profiles) (db11a5f)
- Opus (Ogg) channel pseudo-archive: decodes via Codec.Opus and surfaces FULL.opus + per-channel WAVs (graceful FULL-only fallback); explicit-lookup descriptor (no magic clash with Ogg) (718434f)
- OGG channel split: decode Vorbis/Opus via Codec.Vorbis/Codec.Opus and surface per-channel WAVs (graceful fallback to raw packet blobs) (7464cf8)
- MP3 channel split: decode MPEG audio via Codec.Mp3 and surface per-channel WAVs (graceful FULL-only fallback when the decoder can't handle the input) (ffdc006)
- Wave64 (.w64) channel pseudo-archive: parses GUID-keyed chunks with 64-bit sizes, surfaces FULL.w64 + per-channel WAVs + metadata; IArchiveCreatable writes a Wave64 from per-channel mono WAVs (b24074e)
- Creative Voice (.voc) channel pseudo-archive: walks VOC blocks (legacy type-1 + modern type-9), decodes 8/16-bit PCM, surfaces FULL.voc + per-channel WAVs + text/metadata; IArchiveCreatable writes a .voc from per-channel mono WAVs (fdd8d82)
- RF64 / BWF (Broadcast Wave) channel pseudo-archive: resolves 64-bit sizes via the ds64 chunk, surfaces FULL.rf64 + per-channel WAVs + bext/metadata; IArchiveCreatable writes a conformant RF64 from per-channel mono WAVs (6a6ef75)
- CAF (Apple Core Audio Format) channel pseudo-archive: reads caff/desc/data, surfaces FULL.caf + per-channel WAVs + metadata chunks; IArchiveCreatable assembles a CAF from per-channel mono WAVs (4321749)
- Sun/NeXT .au assemble (IArchiveCreatable): build a multi-channel .au from per-channel mono WAV inputs + AuWriter: 24-byte big-endian header + big-endian linear PCM writer (19b9966)
- AIFF assemble (IArchiveCreatable): build a multi-channel AIFF from per-channel mono WAV inputs + AiffWriter: minimal FORM/AIFF (COMM + SSND) writer with the inverse 80-bit extended-float sample-rate encoder (1ba0d99)
- PcmCodec.Interleave: weave per-channel mono PCM into one interleaved buffer (inverse of SplitInterleavedPcm) — enables assembling multi-channel audio from channels + AudioPseudoArchive helper: shared List/Extract/ExtractEntry projection for audio containers surfaced as channel archives * WavFormatDescriptor.Create uses PcmCodec.Interleave instead of an inline weave loop (9a9e0a8)
